# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

```plantuml
    getFileInfo(filePath : String) : EMap
    getStats(original : String, encoded : String) : EMap

    processFile(inputPath : String, outputPath : String, operation : String) : EMap
    restoreBinaryData(preparedData : PreparedData) : EByteArray
    restoreBinaryDataFromMap(data : EMap) : EByteArray
    suggestRecipe(size : int) : String
    unprocessData(data : EByteArray) : EByteArray
}

    content : EByteArray
    mimeType : String

    hashCode() : int
    toString() : String
}

    createFileData(mimeType : String, content : EByteArray) : FileData

    readFile(filePath : String) : FileData

}

    content : String

    size : int
    toMap() : EMap
}

    none : String
    sha256 : String
}
```

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

```text
Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.
```

# Operation Specifications
FileData::equals
Summary: Tests structural equality with another FileData object.
Algorithm:
  if o is not a FileData: return false
  other = (FileData) o
  return Arrays.equals(content, other.content) && Objects.equals(mimeType, other.mimeType)
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData
Input: o : EJavaObject - object to compare with.
Output: boolean - true if both content and mimeType are equal.
Pre-condition: none.
Post-condition: No field is modified; two FileData objects with equal content and MIME type are equal.

FileData::hashCode
Summary: Computes a hash code based on content and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  return Objects.hash(mimeType, Arrays.hashCode(content))
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData
Input: none
Output: int - hash code of this FileData object.
Pre-condition: none.
Post-condition: Equal FileData objects have equal hash codes.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from a PreparedData object.
Algorithm:
  bytes = Base64.decode(preparedData.content)
  if bytes.length != preparedData.size:
      throw IllegalArgumentException("size mismatch")
  return bytes
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: preparedData : PreparedData - contains Base64 content and original size.
Output: EByteArray - the decoded original binary content.
Pre-condition: preparedData.content is valid Base64; preparedData.size equals the decoded length.
Post-condition: The returned byte array has the same length as preparedData.size.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file metadata and a suggested recipe.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  if file does not exist, is not readable, or is a directory:
      return {"can_process": false, "error": "File unavailable"}
  size = Files.size(path)
  mime = fileHandler.guessMimeType(path)
  recipe = suggestRecipe(size)
  return {"size": size, "mime_type": mime, "can_process": true, "suggested_recipe": recipe}
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler
Input: filePath : String - path of the file to inspect.
Output: EMap - contains size, mime_type, can_process, and suggested_recipe on success; can_process false and error on failure.
Pre-condition: filePath is not null; fileHandler is initialized.
Post-condition: No file data is altered; the returned map describes whether the file can be processed.

EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Suggests a recipe based on file size.
Algorithm:
  if size < 1024: return "quick"
  if size < 10240: return "light"
  if size < 102400: return "classic"
  return "gourmet"
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size is non-negative.
Post-condition: The returned recipe is the suggested recipe for the given size.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Prepares binary data for emoji encoding by base64-encoding the content and preserving MIME type and size.
Algorithm:
  content = Base64.encode(data)
  return new PreparedData(content, mimeType, data.length)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType : String, data : EByteArray
Output: PreparedData - contains Base64 content, MIME type, and original byte size.
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are not null.
Post-condition: preparedData.size == data.length; preparedData.content is valid Base64 text.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the effect of processData, typically decompressing a zlib byte stream.
Algorithm:
  if compression == zlib:
      return zlibDecompress(data)
  else:
      return copy of data
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - processed bytes.
Output: EByteArray - original bytes before processData was applied.
Pre-condition: data was produced by processData with the same compression mode.
Post-condition: Decompresses zlib data when configured; otherwise returns unchanged data.

EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into the original text.
Algorithm:
  if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
  if emojiData == "": return ""
  bits = ""
  for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
      chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
      if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
      append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
  remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
  if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
  encodedBytes = group remaining bits into bytes
  rawBytes = unprocessData(encodedBytes)
  return new String(rawBytes, UTF-8)
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, compression, unprocessData
Related Classes: None
Input: emojiData : String - the emoji-encoded text.
Output: String - the original decoded text; empty string when emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: The active recipe is initialized; compression mode is the same as during encoding; all emoji symbols belong to the active emoji alphabet.
Post-condition: Returns the original text; null input and unknown symbols are rejected.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics for original text and its emoji representation.
Algorithm:
  originalBytes = original.getBytes(UTF_8).length
  encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encoded
  bitsPerEmoji = bitsPerChunk
  theoreticalRatio = 8.0 / bitsPerEmoji
  actualRatio = originalBytes == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) encodedLength / originalBytes
  return {
      "original_bytes": originalBytes,
      "encoded_length": encodedLength,
      "actual_ratio": actualRatio,
      "theoretical_ratio": theoreticalRatio,
      "bits_per_emoji": bitsPerEmoji
  }
Related Features: bitsPerChunk
Related Classes: None
Input: original : String, encoded : String
Output: EMap - the encoding statistics.
Pre-condition: both arguments are non-null; encoded corresponds to original under the active recipe.
Post-condition: No input data is modified; the result map contains all required statistic keys.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.
Algorithm:
  raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)
  if raw.length == 0: return ""
  bytes = processData(raw)
  bits = concatenated bits of bytes, most significant bit first
  append a 1 bit as terminator
  append 0 bits until bits.length is a multiple of bitsPerChunk
  result = ""
  for each chunk of bitsPerChunk bits:
      value = integer value of chunk
      result += emojiMap.get(value)
  return result
Related Features: compression, bitsPerChunk, emojiMap, recipeType, baseSize, mask, processData
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : String - the text to encode.
Output: String - an emoji-encoded string; empty string if data is empty.
Pre-condition: data is not null; the recipe is initialized; emojiMap and reverseMap are populated; compression mode is set.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded back to the original text; empty input round-trips as empty output.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Computes an optional SHA-256 integrity digest for a byte array.
Algorithm:
  if verification mode is "sha256":
      digest = SHA-256(data)
      return lowercaseHex(digest) left-padded with zeros to 64 characters
  else:
      return ""
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash.
Output: String - a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64 when mode is "sha256", otherwise an empty string.
Pre-condition: data is not null; verification mode is either "none" or "sha256".
Post-condition: No data is modified; the returned digest deterministically represents the input.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji-encoded binary wrapper back into a byte array and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  jsonText = decode(encoded)
  map = parseJson(jsonText)
  bytes = restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map)
  mime = (String) map.get("mime_type")
  return list [bytes, mime]
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: encoded : String - the emoji string produced by encodeBinary.
Output: List<EJavaObject> with two elements: element 0 is EByteArray with the restored binary data, element 1 is String with the MIME type.
Pre-condition: encoded was produced by encodeBinary and contains a valid JSON object with content, mime_type, and size.
Post-condition: The original binary bytes and MIME type are restored.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Encodes binary data by wrapping it in a JSON-compatible structure and then emoji-encoding the JSON text.
Algorithm:
  prepared = prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data)
  map = prepared.toMap()
  json = toJson(map)
  return encode(json)
Related Features: prepareBinaryData, encode
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType : String, data : EByteArray
Output: String - an emoji string representing the binary wrapper.
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are not null; codec is initialized.
Post-condition: The output contains serialized fields content, mime_type, and size; it can be decoded by decodeBinary.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  if compression == zlib:
      return zlibCompress(data)
  else:
      return copy of data
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - compressed bytes when zlib mode is active, otherwise unchanged bytes.
Pre-condition: compression mode is "none" or "zlib"; data is not null.
Post-condition: The returned bytes can be reversed by unprocessData with the same compression mode.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Encodes or decodes a file and writes the result to an output path.
Algorithm:
  if operation == "encode":
      fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
      encoded = encodeBinary(fileData.mimeType, fileData.content)
      fileHandler.writeFile(outputPath, encoded.getBytes(UTF_8))
      return {
          "original_bytes": fileData.content.length,
          "encoded_length": countEmojiSymbols(encoded),
          "mime_type": fileData.mimeType,
          "actual_ratio": original_bytes == 0 ? 0.0 : encoded_length / original_bytes
      }
  else if operation == "decode":
      fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
      emojiText = new String(fileData.content, UTF_8)
      decoded = decodeBinary(emojiText)
      bytes = decoded.get(0)
      mime = decoded.get(1)
      fileHandler.writeFile(outputPath, bytes)
      return {"decoded_size": bytes.length, "mime_type": mime}
  else:
      throw IllegalArgumentException
Related Features: fileHandler, encodeBinary, decodeBinary
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: inputPath : String, outputPath : String, operation : String
Output: EMap - encoding or decoding result information.
Pre-condition: operation is "encode" or "decode"; input file exists and is readable; output parent directories may be missing.
Post-condition: For encode, an emoji text file is written. For decode, the original binary file is written. Missing parent directories are created.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores binary bytes from a map containing the JSON fields content, mime_type, and size.
Algorithm:
  content = (String) data.get("content")
  mime = (String) data.get("mime_type")
  size = ((Number) data.get("size")).intValue()
  return restoreBinaryData(new PreparedData(content, mime, size))
Related Features: restoreBinaryData
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data : EMap - map with content, mime_type, and size entries.
Output: EByteArray - the original binary content.
Pre-condition: data contains the required fields; content is valid Base64; size matches decoded length.
Post-condition: Returns the original binary data without modifying the input map.

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from a PreparedData object.
Algorithm:
  bytes = Base64.decode(preparedData.content)
  if bytes.length != preparedData.size:
      throw IllegalArgumentException("size mismatch")
  return bytes
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: preparedData : PreparedData - contains Base64 content and original size.
Output: EByteArray - the decoded original binary content.
Pre-condition: preparedData.content is valid Base64; preparedData.size equals the decoded length.
Post-condition: The returned byte array has the same length as preparedData.size.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file metadata and a suggested recipe.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  if file does not exist, is not readable, or is a directory:
      return {"can_process": false, "error": "File unavailable"}
  size = Files.size(path)
  mime = fileHandler.guessMimeType(path)
  recipe = suggestRecipe(size)
  return {"size": size, "mime_type": mime, "can_process": true, "suggested_recipe": recipe}
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler
Input: filePath : String - path of the file to inspect.
Output: EMap - contains size, mime_type, can_process, and suggested_recipe on success; can_process false and error on failure.
Pre-condition: filePath is not null; fileHandler is initialized.
Post-condition: No file data is altered; the returned map describes whether the file can be processed.

EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Suggests a recipe based on file size.
Algorithm:
  if size < 1024: return "quick"
  if size < 10240: return "light"
  if size < 102400: return "classic"
  return "gourmet"
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size is non-negative.
Post-condition: The returned recipe is the suggested recipe for the given size.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Prepares binary data for emoji encoding by base64-encoding the content and preserving MIME type and size.
Algorithm:
  content = Base64.encode(data)
  return new PreparedData(content, mimeType, data.length)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType : String, data : EByteArray
Output: PreparedData - contains Base64 content, MIME type, and original byte size.
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are not null.
Post-condition: preparedData.size == data.length; preparedData.content is valid Base64 text.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the effect of processData, typically decompressing a zlib byte stream.
Algorithm:
  if compression == zlib:
      return zlibDecompress(data)
  else:
      return copy of data
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - processed bytes.
Output: EByteArray - original bytes before processData was applied.
Pre-condition: data was produced by processData with the same compression mode.
Post-condition: Decompresses zlib data when configured; otherwise returns unchanged data.

EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into the original text.
Algorithm:
  if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
  if emojiData == "": return ""
  bits = ""
  for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
      chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
      if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
      append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
  remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
  if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
  encodedBytes = group remaining bits into bytes
  rawBytes = unprocessData(encodedBytes)
  return new String(rawBytes, UTF-8)
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, compression, unprocessData
Related Classes: None
Input: emojiData : String - the emoji-encoded text.
Output: String - the original decoded text; empty string when emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: The active recipe is initialized; compression mode is the same as during encoding; all emoji symbols belong to the active emoji alphabet.
Post-condition: Returns the original text; null input and unknown symbols are rejected.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics for original text and its emoji representation.
Algorithm:
  originalBytes = original.getBytes(UTF_8).length
  encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encoded
  bitsPerEmoji = bitsPerChunk
  theoreticalRatio = 8.0 / bitsPerEmoji
  actualRatio = originalBytes == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) encodedLength / originalBytes
  return {
      "original_bytes": originalBytes,
      "encoded_length": encodedLength,
      "actual_ratio": actualRatio,
      "theoretical_ratio": theoreticalRatio,
      "bits_per_emoji": bitsPerEmoji
  }
Related Features: bitsPerChunk
Related Classes: None
Input: original : String, encoded : String
Output: EMap - the encoding statistics.
Pre-condition: both arguments are non-null; encoded corresponds to original under the active recipe.
Post-condition: No input data is modified; the result map contains all required statistic keys.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.
Algorithm:
  raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)
  if raw.length == 0: return ""
  bytes = processData(raw)
  bits = concatenated bits of bytes, most significant bit first
  append a 1 bit as terminator
  append 0 bits until bits.length is a multiple of bitsPerChunk
  result = ""
  for each chunk of bitsPerChunk bits:
      value = integer value of chunk
      result += emojiMap.get(value)
  return result
Related Features: compression, bitsPerChunk, emojiMap, recipeType, baseSize, mask, processData
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : String - the text to encode.
Output: String - an emoji-encoded string; empty string if data is empty.
Pre-condition: data is not null; the recipe is initialized; emojiMap and reverseMap are populated; compression mode is set.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded back to the original text; empty input round-trips as empty output.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Computes an optional SHA-256 integrity digest for a byte array.
Algorithm:
  if verification mode is "sha256":
      digest = SHA-256(data)
      return lowercaseHex(digest) left-padded with zeros to 64 characters
  else:
      return ""
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash.
Output: String - a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64 when mode is "sha256", otherwise an empty string.
Pre-condition: data is not null; verification mode is either "none" or "sha256".
Post-condition: No data is modified; the returned digest deterministically represents the input.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji-encoded binary wrapper back into a byte array and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  jsonText = decode(encoded)
  map = parseJson(jsonText)
  bytes = restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map)
  mime = (String) map.get("mime_type")
  return list [bytes, mime]
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: encoded : String - the emoji string produced by encodeBinary.
Output: List<EJavaObject> with two elements: element 0 is EByteArray with the restored binary data, element 1 is String with the MIME type.
Pre-condition: encoded was produced by encodeBinary and contains a valid JSON object with content, mime_type, and size.
Post-condition: The original binary bytes and MIME type are restored.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Encodes binary data by wrapping it in a JSON-compatible structure and then emoji-encoding the JSON text.
Algorithm:
  prepared = prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data)
  map = prepared.toMap()
  json = toJson(map)
  return encode(json)
Related Features: prepareBinaryData, encode
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType : String, data : EByteArray
Output: String - an emoji string representing the binary wrapper.
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are not null; codec is initialized.
Post-condition: The output contains serialized fields content, mime_type, and size; it can be decoded by decodeBinary.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  if compression == zlib:
      return zlibCompress(data)
  else:
      return copy of data
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - compressed bytes when zlib mode is active, otherwise unchanged bytes.
Pre-condition: compression mode is "none" or "zlib"; data is not null.
Post-condition: The returned bytes can be reversed by unprocessData with the same compression mode.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Encodes or decodes a file and writes the result to an output path.
Algorithm:
  if operation == "encode":
      fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
      encoded = encodeBinary(fileData.mimeType, fileData.content)
      fileHandler.writeFile(outputPath, encoded.getBytes(UTF_8))
      return {
          "original_bytes": fileData.content.length,
          "encoded_length": countEmojiSymbols(encoded),
          "mime_type": fileData.mimeType,
          "actual_ratio": original_bytes == 0 ? 0.0 : encoded_length / original_bytes
      }
  else if operation == "decode":
      fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
      emojiText = new String(fileData.content, UTF_8)
      decoded = decodeBinary(emojiText)
      bytes = decoded.get(0)
      mime = decoded.get(1)
      fileHandler.writeFile(outputPath, bytes)
      return {"decoded_size": bytes.length, "mime_type": mime}
  else:
      throw IllegalArgumentException
Related Features: fileHandler, encodeBinary, decodeBinary
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: inputPath : String, outputPath : String, operation : String
Output: EMap - encoding or decoding result information.
Pre-condition: operation is "encode" or "decode"; input file exists and is readable; output parent directories may be missing.
Post-condition: For encode, an emoji text file is written. For decode, the original binary file is written. Missing parent directories are created.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores binary bytes from a map containing the JSON fields content, mime_type, and size.
Algorithm:
  content = (String) data.get("content")
  mime = (String) data.get("mime_type")
  size = ((Number) data.get("size")).intValue()
  return restoreBinaryData(new PreparedData(content, mime, size))
Related Features: restoreBinaryData
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data : EMap - map with content, mime_type, and size entries.
Output: EByteArray - the original binary content.
Pre-condition: data contains the required fields; content is valid Base64; size matches decoded length.
Post-condition: Returns the original binary data without modifying the input map.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

  content = (String) data.get("content")
  mime = (String) data.get("mime_type")
  size = ((Number) data.get("size")).intValue()
  return restoreBinaryData(new PreparedData(content, mime, size))
Related Features: restoreBinaryData
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data : EMap - map with content, mime_type, and size entries.
Output: EByteArray - the original binary content.
Pre-condition: data contains the required fields; content is valid Base64; size matches decoded length.
Post-condition: Returns the original binary data without modifying the input map.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores original binary bytes from a PreparedData object.
Algorithm:
  bytes = Base64.decode(preparedData.content)
  if bytes.length != preparedData.size:
      throw IllegalArgumentException("size mismatch")
  return bytes
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: preparedData : PreparedData - contains Base64 content and original size.
Output: EByteArray - the decoded original binary content.
Pre-condition: preparedData.content is valid Base64; preparedData.size equals the decoded length.
Post-condition: The returned byte array has the same length as preparedData.size.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Returns file metadata and a suggested recipe.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  if file does not exist, is not readable, or is a directory:
      return {"can_process": false, "error": "File unavailable"}
  size = Files.size(path)
  mime = fileHandler.guessMimeType(path)
  recipe = suggestRecipe(size)
  return {"size": size, "mime_type": mime, "can_process": true, "suggested_recipe": recipe}
Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
Related Classes: FileHandler
Input: filePath : String - path of the file to inspect.
Output: EMap - contains size, mime_type, can_process, and suggested_recipe on success; can_process false and error on failure.
Pre-condition: filePath is not null; fileHandler is initialized.
Post-condition: No file data is altered; the returned map describes whether the file can be processed.

EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Suggests a recipe based on file size.
Algorithm:
  if size < 1024: return "quick"
  if size < 10240: return "light"
  if size < 102400: return "classic"
  return "gourmet"
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: size : int - file size in bytes.
Output: String - one of "quick", "light", "classic", or "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size is non-negative.
Post-condition: The returned recipe is the suggested recipe for the given size.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Prepares binary data for emoji encoding by base64-encoding the content and preserving MIME type and size.
Algorithm:
  content = Base64.encode(data)
  return new PreparedData(content, mimeType, data.length)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType : String, data : EByteArray
Output: PreparedData - contains Base64 content, MIME type, and original byte size.
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are not null.
Post-condition: preparedData.size == data.length; preparedData.content is valid Base64 text.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the effect of processData, typically decompressing a zlib byte stream.
Algorithm:
  if compression == zlib:
      return zlibDecompress(data)
  else:
      return copy of data
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - processed bytes.
Output: EByteArray - original bytes before processData was applied.
Pre-condition: data was produced by processData with the same compression mode.
Post-condition: Decompresses zlib data when configured; otherwise returns unchanged data.

EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into the original text.
Algorithm:
  if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
  if emojiData == "": return ""
  bits = ""
  for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
      chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
      if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
      append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
  remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
  if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
  encodedBytes = group remaining bits into bytes
  rawBytes = unprocessData(encodedBytes)
  return new String(rawBytes, UTF-8)
Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, compression, unprocessData
Related Classes: None
Input: emojiData : String - the emoji-encoded text.
Output: String - the original decoded text; empty string when emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: The active recipe is initialized; compression mode is the same as during encoding; all emoji symbols belong to the active emoji alphabet.
Post-condition: Returns the original text; null input and unknown symbols are rejected.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics for original text and its emoji representation.
Algorithm:
  originalBytes = original.getBytes(UTF_8).length
  encodedLength = number of emoji symbols in encoded
  bitsPerEmoji = bitsPerChunk
  theoreticalRatio = 8.0 / bitsPerEmoji
  actualRatio = originalBytes == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) encodedLength / originalBytes
  return {
      "original_bytes": originalBytes,
      "encoded_length": encodedLength,
      "actual_ratio": actualRatio,
      "theoretical_ratio": theoreticalRatio,
      "bits_per_emoji": bitsPerEmoji
  }
Related Features: bitsPerChunk
Related Classes: None
Input: original : String, encoded : String
Output: EMap - the encoding statistics.
Pre-condition: both arguments are non-null; encoded corresponds to original under the active recipe.
Post-condition: No input data is modified; the result map contains all required statistic keys.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.
Algorithm:
  raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)
  if raw.length == 0: return ""
  bytes = processData(raw)
  bits = concatenated bits of bytes, most significant bit first
  append a 1 bit as terminator
  append 0 bits until bits.length is a multiple of bitsPerChunk
  result = ""
  for each chunk of bitsPerChunk bits:
      value = integer value of chunk
      result += emojiMap.get(value)
  return result
Related Features: compression, bitsPerChunk, emojiMap, recipeType, baseSize, mask, processData
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : String - the text to encode.
Output: String - an emoji-encoded string; empty string if data is empty.
Pre-condition: data is not null; the recipe is initialized; emojiMap and reverseMap are populated; compression mode is set.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded back to the original text; empty input round-trips as empty output.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Computes an optional SHA-256 integrity digest for a byte array.
Algorithm:
  if verification mode is "sha256":
      digest = SHA-256(data)
      return lowercaseHex(digest) left-padded with zeros to 64 characters
  else:
      return ""
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to hash.
Output: String - a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of length 64 when mode is "sha256", otherwise an empty string.
Pre-condition: data is not null; verification mode is either "none" or "sha256".
Post-condition: No data is modified; the returned digest deterministically represents the input.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji-encoded binary wrapper back into a byte array and MIME type.
Algorithm:
  jsonText = decode(encoded)
  map = parseJson(jsonText)
  bytes = restoreBinaryDataFromMap(map)
  mime = (String) map.get("mime_type")
  return list [bytes, mime]
Related Features: decode, restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: encoded : String - the emoji string produced by encodeBinary.
Output: List<EJavaObject> with two elements: element 0 is EByteArray with the restored binary data, element 1 is String with the MIME type.
Pre-condition: encoded was produced by encodeBinary and contains a valid JSON object with content, mime_type, and size.
Post-condition: The original binary bytes and MIME type are restored.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Encodes binary data by wrapping it in a JSON-compatible structure and then emoji-encoding the JSON text.
Algorithm:
  prepared = prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data)
  map = prepared.toMap()
  json = toJson(map)
  return encode(json)
Related Features: prepareBinaryData, encode
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType : String, data : EByteArray
Output: String - an emoji string representing the binary wrapper.
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are not null; codec is initialized.
Post-condition: The output contains serialized fields content, mime_type, and size; it can be decoded by decodeBinary.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression mode to a byte array.
Algorithm:
  if compression == zlib:
      return zlibCompress(data)
  else:
      return copy of data
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data : EByteArray - the bytes to process.
Output: EByteArray - compressed bytes when zlib mode is active, otherwise unchanged bytes.
Pre-condition: compression mode is "none" or "zlib"; data is not null.
Post-condition: The returned bytes can be reversed by unprocessData with the same compression mode.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Encodes or decodes a file and writes the result to an output path.
Algorithm:
  if operation == "encode":
      fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
      encoded = encodeBinary(fileData.mimeType, fileData.content)
      fileHandler.writeFile(outputPath, encoded.getBytes(UTF_8))
      return {
          "original_bytes": fileData.content.length,
          "encoded_length": countEmojiSymbols(encoded),
          "mime_type": fileData.mimeType,
          "actual_ratio": original_bytes == 0 ? 0.0 : encoded_length / original_bytes
      }
  else if operation == "decode":
      fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
      emojiText = new String(fileData.content, UTF_8)
      decoded = decodeBinary(emojiText)
      bytes = decoded.get(0)
      mime = decoded.get(1)
      fileHandler.writeFile(outputPath, bytes)
      return {"decoded_size": bytes.length, "mime_type": mime}
  else:
      throw IllegalArgumentException
Related Features: fileHandler, encodeBinary, decodeBinary
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: inputPath : String, outputPath : String, operation : String
Output: EMap - encoding or decoding result information.
Pre-condition: operation is "encode" or "decode"; input file exists and is readable; output parent directories may be missing.
Post-condition: For encode, an emoji text file is written. For decode, the original binary file is written. Missing parent directories are created.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores binary bytes from a map containing the JSON fields content, mime_type, and size.
Algorithm:
  content = (String) data.get("content")
  mime = (String) data.get("mime_type")
  size = ((Number) data.get("size")).intValue()
  return restoreBinaryData(new PreparedData(content, mime, size))
Related Features: restoreBinaryData
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data : EMap - map with content, mime_type, and size entries.
Output: EByteArray - the original binary content.
Pre-condition: data contains the required fields; content is valid Base64; size matches decoded length.
Post-condition: Returns the original binary data without modifying the input map.

# Operation Specifications
FileHandler::createFileData
Summary: Creates a FileData object with the given MIME type and content.
Algorithm:
  return new FileData(content, mimeType)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: FileData
Input: mimeType : String, content : EByteArray
Output: FileData - a new object containing the supplied values.
Pre-condition: mimeType and content are not null.
Post-condition: returned.content is the supplied content; returned.mimeType is the supplied MIME type.

FileHandler::readFile
Summary: Reads raw file bytes and guesses the MIME type.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  mime = guessMimeType(path)
  bytes = Files.readAllBytes(path)
  return createFileData(mime, bytes)
Related Features: guessMimeType, createFileData
Related Classes: FileData
Input: filePath : String - path of the file to read.
Output: FileData - contains the raw bytes and guessed MIME type.
Pre-condition: filePath exists and is readable.
Post-condition: returned.content holds the raw file bytes; returned.mimeType is set.

FileHandler::writeFile
Summary: Writes bytes to a file, creating missing parent directories.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  parent = path.getParent()
  if parent != null && !exists(parent):
      createDirectories(parent)
  Files.write(path, content)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: filePath : String, content : EByteArray
Output: void
Pre-condition: filePath and content are not null.
Post-condition: The file exists at filePath and contains content; parent directories are created if needed.

FileHandler::guessMimeType
Summary: Infers MIME type from a file name extension, with a fallback.
Algorithm:
  name = path.getFileName().toString().toLowerCase()
  ext = substring after last '.'
  return extensionToMimeMap.getOrDefault(ext, "application/octet-stream")
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: path : Path - file path whose MIME type should be guessed.
Output: String - MIME type, e.g. "text/plain", "image/png"; "application/octet-stream" if unknown.

Post-condition: The returned MIME type is inferred from the extension or is the octet-stream fallback.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
FileHandler::createFileData
Summary: Creates a FileData object with the given MIME type and content.
Algorithm:
  return new FileData(content, mimeType)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: FileData
Input: mimeType : String, content : EByteArray
Output: FileData - a new object containing the supplied values.
Pre-condition: mimeType and content are not null.
Post-condition: returned.content is the supplied content; returned.mimeType is the supplied MIME type.

FileHandler::readFile
Summary: Reads raw file bytes and guesses the MIME type.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  mime = guessMimeType(path)
  bytes = Files.readAllBytes(path)
  return createFileData(mime, bytes)
Related Features: guessMimeType, createFileData
Related Classes: FileData
Input: filePath : String - path of the file to read.
Output: FileData - contains the raw bytes and guessed MIME type.
Pre-condition: filePath exists and is readable.
Post-condition: returned.content holds the raw file bytes; returned.mimeType is set.

FileHandler::writeFile
Summary: Writes bytes to a file, creating missing parent directories.
Algorithm:
  path = Paths.get(filePath)
  parent = path.getParent()
  if parent != null && !exists(parent):
      createDirectories(parent)
  Files.write(path, content)
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: filePath : String, content : EByteArray
Output: void
Pre-condition: filePath and content are not null.
Post-condition: The file exists at filePath and contains content; parent directories are created if needed.

FileHandler::guessMimeType
Summary: Infers MIME type from a file name extension, with a fallback.
Algorithm:
  name = path.getFileName().toString().toLowerCase()
  ext = substring after last '.'
  return extensionToMimeMap.getOrDefault(ext, "application/octet-stream")
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: path : Path - file path whose MIME type should be guessed.
Output: String - MIME type, e.g. "text/plain", "image/png"; "application/octet-stream" if unknown.
Pre-condition: path is not null.
Post-condition: The returned MIME type is inferred from the extension or is the octet-stream fallback.

```java
   *   bytes = processData(raw)

   * Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into the original text.
   * Algorithm:
   *   if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   if emojiData == "": return ""
   *   bits = ""
   *   for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
   *       chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
   *       if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
   *       append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
   *   remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
   *   if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   encodedBytes = group remaining bits into bytes

   *   return new String(rawBytes, UTF-8)
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

```java
   * Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.

   *   raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)

   * Algorithm:
   *   if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   if emojiData == "": return ""
   *   bits = ""
   *   for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
   *       chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
   *       if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
   *       append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
   *   remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
   *   if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
```

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.

   *   raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)
   *   if raw.length == 0: return ""
   *   bytes = processData(raw)
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

```java
   * Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.
   * Algorithm:
   *   raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)
   *   if raw.length == 0: return ""
   *   bytes = processData(raw)
   *   bits = concatenated bits of bytes, most significant bit first
   *   append a 1 bit as terminator
   *   append 0 bits until bits.length is a multiple of bitsPerChunk
   *   result = ""

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into the original text.
   * Algorithm:
   *   if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   if emojiData == "": return ""
   *   bits = ""
   *   for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
   *       chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
   *       if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
   *       append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
   *   remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
   *   if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   encodedBytes = group remaining bits into bytes
   *   rawBytes = unprocessData(encodedBytes)
   *   return new String(rawBytes, UTF-8)
   * Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, compression, unprocessData
   * Related Classes: None
```

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Encodes text into an emoji string using the active recipe.
   * Algorithm:
   *   raw = data.getBytes(UTF_8)
   *   if raw.length == 0: return ""
   *   bytes = processData(raw)
   *   bits = concatenated bits of bytes, most significant bit first
   *   append a 1 bit as terminator
   *   append 0 bits until bits.length is a multiple of bitsPerChunk
   *   result = ""
   *   for each chunk of bitsPerChunk bits:
   *       value = integer value of chunk
   *       result += emojiMap.get(value)
   *   return result
   * Related Features: compression, bitsPerChunk, emojiMap, recipeType, baseSize, mask, processData
   * Related Classes: CompressionMethod
   * Input: data : String - the text to encode.
   * Output: String - an emoji-encoded string; empty string if data is empty.
   * Pre-condition: data is not null; the recipe is initialized; emojiMap and reverseMap are populated; compression mode is set.
   * Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded back to the original text; empty input round-trips as empty output.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String encode(final String data)
  {
    byte[] raw = data.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
    if (raw.length == 0)
    {
      return "";
    }
    byte[] bytes = processData(raw);

    StringBuilder bits = new StringBuilder();
    for (byte b : bytes)
    {
      for (int bit = 7; bit >= 0; bit--)
      {
        bits.append(((b >> bit) & 1) == 0 ? '0' : '1');
      }
    }
    bits.append('1');

    int chunkSize = getBitsPerChunk();
    if (chunkSize <= 0)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("bitsPerChunk must be positive");
    }
    int remainder = bits.length() % chunkSize;
    if (remainder != 0)
    {
      for (int i = 0; i < chunkSize - remainder; i++)
      {
        bits.append('0');
      }
    }

    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder();
    for (int pos = 0; pos < bits.length(); pos += chunkSize)
    {
      String chunkBits = bits.substring(pos, pos + chunkSize);
      int value = (int) Long.parseLong(chunkBits, 2);
      result.append(getEmojiMap().get(value));
    }
    return result.toString();
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into the original text.
   * Algorithm:
   *   if emojiData == null: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   if emojiData == "": return ""
   *   bits = ""
   *   for each emoji symbol in emojiData:
   *       chunk = reverseMap.get(symbol)
   *       if chunk == null: throw IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol")
   *       append chunk as a bitsPerChunk-bit binary value to bits
   *   remove trailing zero bits until a 1 bit is found; remove that 1 bit
   *   if remaining bit count is not a multiple of 8: throw IllegalArgumentException
   *   encodedBytes = group remaining bits into bytes
   *   rawBytes = unprocessData(encodedBytes)
   *   return new String(rawBytes, UTF-8)
   * Related Features: reverseMap, bitsPerChunk, compression, unprocessData
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input: emojiData : String - the emoji-encoded text.
   * Output: String - the original decoded text; empty string when emojiData is empty.
   * Pre-condition: The active recipe is initialized; compression mode is the same as during encoding; all emoji symbols belong to the active emoji alphabet.
   * Post-condition: Returns the original text; null input and unknown symbols are rejected.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String decode(final String emojiData)
  {
    if (emojiData == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("emojiData must not be null");
    }
    if (emojiData.isEmpty())
    {
      return "";
    }

    int chunkSize = getBitsPerChunk();
    if (chunkSize <= 0)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("bitsPerChunk must be positive");
    }
    Map<String, Integer> rev = getReverseMap();

    StringBuilder bits = new StringBuilder();
    int i = 0;
    while (i < emojiData.length())
    {
      String symbol = null;
      Integer chunk = null;
      for (String key : rev.keySet())
      {
        if (key != null && key.length() > 0 && emojiData.startsWith(key, i))
        {
          if (symbol == null || key.length() > symbol.length())
          {
            symbol = key;
            chunk = rev.get(key);
          }
        }
      }
      if (symbol == null)
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("unknown symbol");
      }
      String bin = Integer.toBinaryString(chunk);
      while (bin.length() < chunkSize)
      {
        bin = "0" + bin;
      }
      bits.append(bin);
      i += symbol.length();
    }

    int end = bits.length();
    while (end > 0 && bits.charAt(end - 1) == '0')
    {
      end--;
    }
    if (end == 0)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("missing terminator bit");
    }
    end--;
    bits.setLength(end);

    if (bits.length() % 8 != 0)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("invalid bit length");
    }

    byte[] encodedBytes = new byte[bits.length() / 8];
    for (int j = 0; j < encodedBytes.length; j++)
    {
      String byteBits = bits.substring(j * 8, j * 8 + 8);
      encodedBytes[j] = (byte) Integer.parseInt(byteBits, 2);
    }

    byte[] rawBytes = unprocessData(encodedBytes);
    return new String(rawBytes, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
  }

  public Map<String, Double> getStats(final String original, final String encoded)
  {
    int originalBytes = original.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8).length;
    int encodedLength = countEmojiSymbols(encoded);
    int bitsPerEmoji = getBitsPerChunk();

    double theoreticalRatio = 8.0 / bitsPerEmoji;
    double actualRatio = originalBytes == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) encodedLength / originalBytes;

    Map<String, Double> result = new HashMap<String, Double>();
    result.put("original_bytes", (double) originalBytes);
    result.put("encoded_length", (double) encodedLength);
    result.put("actual_ratio", actualRatio);
    result.put("theoretical_ratio", theoreticalRatio);

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Encodes or decodes a file and writes the result to an output path.
   * Algorithm:
   *   if operation == "encode":
   *       fileData = fileHandler.readFile(inputPath)
   *       encoded = encodeBinary(fileData.mimeType, fileData.content)

   *       }

   *       decoded = decodeBinary(emojiText)

   * Post-condition: For encode, an emoji text file is written. For decode, the original binary file is written. Missing parent directories are created.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<String, Object> processFile(final String inputPath, final String outputPath, final String operation)
  {
    if ("encode".equals(operation))
    {
      FileData fileData = getFileHandler().readFile(inputPath);
      String encoded = encodeBinary(fileData.getMimeType(), fileData.getContent());
      int originalBytes = fileData.getContent().length;
      int encodedLength = countEmojiSymbols(encoded);
      getFileHandler().writeFile(outputPath, encoded.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));

      Map<String, Object> result = new HashMap<String, Object>();
      result.put("original_bytes", originalBytes);
      result.put("encoded_length", encodedLength);
      result.put("mime_type", fileData.getMimeType());
      result.put("actual_ratio", originalBytes == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) encodedLength / originalBytes);
      return result;
    }
    else if ("decode".equals(operation))
    {
      FileData fileData = getFileHandler().readFile(inputPath);
      String emojiText = new String(fileData.getContent(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
      EList<Object> decoded = decodeBinary(emojiText);
      byte[] bytes = (byte[]) decoded.get(0);
      String mime = (String) decoded.get(1);
      getFileHandler().writeFile(outputPath, bytes);

      Map<String, Object> result = new HashMap<String, Object>();
      result.put("decoded_size", bytes.length);
      result.put("mime_type", mime);
      return result;
    }
    else
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Unknown operation: " + operation);
    }
  }

   * Algorithm:
   *   path = Paths.get(filePath)
   *   if file does not exist, is not readable, or is a directory:
   *       return {"can_process": false, "error": "File unavailable"}
   *   size = Files.size(path)
   *   mime = fileHandler.guessMimeType(path)
   *   recipe = suggestRecipe(size)
   *   return {"size": size, "mime_type": mime, "can_process": true, "suggested_recipe": recipe}
   * Related Features: fileHandler, suggestRecipe
   * Related Classes: FileHandler
   * Input: filePath : String - path of the file to inspect.
   * Output: EMap - contains size, mime_type, can_process, and suggested_recipe on success; can_process false and error on failure.
   * Pre-condition: filePath is not null; fileHandler is initialized.
   * Post-condition: No file data is altered; the returned map describes whether the file can be processed.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<String, Object> getFileInfo(final String filePath)
  {
    Path path = Paths.get(filePath);
    try
    {
      if (!Files.exists(path) || !Files.isReadable(path) || Files.isDirectory(path))
      {
        Map<String, Object> errorResult = new HashMap<String, Object>();
        errorResult.put("can_process", false);
        errorResult.put("error", "File unavailable");
        return errorResult;
      }

      long size = Files.size(path);
      String mime = getFileHandler().guessMimeType(path);
      String recipe = suggestRecipe((int) size);

      Map<String, Object> result = new HashMap<String, Object>();
      result.put("size", size);
      result.put("mime_type", mime);
      result.put("can_process", true);
      result.put("suggested_recipe", recipe);
      return result;
    }
    catch (Exception e)
    {
      Map<String, Object> errorResult = new HashMap<String, Object>();
      errorResult.put("can_process", false);
      errorResult.put("error", "File unavailable");
      return errorResult;
    }
  }
